People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

that I keep hearing water running and men whispering inside the walls when nobody’s there, and sometimes I see a dark wet patch spreading across the hallway even after I’ve checked and it’s dry.\n- diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to be the guy who showed up for soccer, brewed beer in the garage, and helped at the fire department, but now all of it feels pointless and I can’t make myself care enough to go.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Food mostly tastes like cardboard, so I’ll skip meals without noticing and my jeans have gotten loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in I wasn’t thinking the neighbors were watching me like that.\n\nThat all came after I’d already stopped caring about my normal life and was feeling like a failure most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to. Food just doesn’t taste like much anymore, kind of like cardboard, so I skip meals without really noticing. My jeans are loose now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and you weren't able to sleep because you felt like you had so much energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had good moods before, like anybody, but not where I was full of energy and didn’t need sleep or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
```json
  }

And it just kind of sits there on me all day, until I go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to be the guy who’d show up for soccer, mess around brewing beer in the garage, help out at the fire department… stuff like that.\n\nNow it just feels pointless. I can’t really make myself care enough to go.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and visions stop when you felt slightly better, or have they continued even on days when your low mood wasn't as bad?\n\nPatient: They didn’t really stop just because I had a slightly better day.\n\nI mean… before all this with the house dragging on, before I got this low, I wasn’t hearing things in the walls or seeing wet spots that weren’t there. I wasn’t thinking the neighbors were watching me like that.\n\nThat all came after I’d already stopped caring about my normal life and was feeling like a failure most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to. Food just doesn’t taste like much anymore, kind of like cardboard, so I skip meals without really noticing. My jeans are loose now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and you weren't able to sleep because you felt like you had so much energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had good moods before, like anybody, but not where I was full of energy and didn’t need sleep or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt extremely happy or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had anything like that for several days. Not that I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy or energetic for at least several days?\n\nPatient: No… I already said, I don’t really remember anything like that. Not for days at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely energetic or irritable for more than a few days at any point in your life before these past seven months?\n\nPatient: No… before this, I don’t remember any stretch like that. Not more than a few days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Then the other stuff showed up.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel low and stop caring about things before you started hearing the voices and seeing things that aren't there, or did they happen at the same time?\n\nPatient: The low part came first. I’d already stopped caring about… normal things, and I felt like I was failing every day. The voices and seeing things, that came after. I didn’t have that before.\n\nDoctor: How would you describe the intensity of your feelings of failing every day and not caring about things?\n\nPatient: It’s… pretty heavy. Most days I wake up already feeling like I’ve failed as a husband, as a man. And it just kind of sits there on me all day, until I go to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to be the guy who’d show up for soccer, mess around brewing beer in the garage, help out at the fire department… stuff like that.\n\nNow it just feels pointless. I can’t really make myself care enough to go.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices and visions stop when you felt slightly better, or have they continued even on days when your low mood wasn't as bad?\n\nPatient: They didn’t really stop just because I had a slightly better day.\n\nI mean… before all this with the house dragging on, before I got this low, I wasn’t hearing things in the walls or seeing wet spots that weren’t there. I wasn’t thinking the neighbors were watching me like that.\n\nThat all came after I’d already stopped caring about my normal life and was feeling like a failure most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been trying to. Food just doesn’t taste like much anymore, kind of like cardboard, so I skip meals without really noticing. My jeans are loose now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy, energetic, or irritable, and you weren't able to sleep because you felt like you had so much energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had good moods before, like anybody, but not where I was full of energy and didn’t need sleep or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt extremely happy or energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had anything like that for several days. Not that I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy or energetic for at least several days?\n\nPatient: No… I already said, I don’t really remember anything like that. Not for days at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely energetic or irritable for more than a few days at any point in your life before these past seven months?\n\nPatient: No… before this, I don’t remember any stretch like that. Not more than a few days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely irritable for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I get irritated like anyone, but not for several days like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely happy or energetic and your friends or family noticed a change in you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.     "functional_impairment": true,

    "psychosocial_stressor": true,
    "additional_requirements": [
      "onset of depressive symptoms prior to psychotic symptoms",
      "no history of manic or hypomanic episodes"
    ]
  }
}
```
